- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 8
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10

        - MinorCivWarBias: 5
        - DenounceWillingness: 9
        - Forgiveness: 5
        - Meanness: 5
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 2
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Reduce Meanness slightly to leave room for potential diplomatic deals with other civs (e.g., trade routes, open borders) while maintaining overall aggressive posture toward India and Zulus.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: New World Order (Policy)
        - Rationale: New World Order will give immediate -2 Unhappiness (critical with current 38% happiness and combat penalty) and boost production and culture from Police Stations/Constabularies, helping to finish gold‑generating buildings faster.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Korea
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Korea has capitulated and become our vassal, adopting Catholicism across their cities. Previous maximum hostility modifiers (-100) are now counterproductive. Resetting to 0 allows natural vassal-master relationship bonuses to function, enabling continued tribute, trade routes, and strategic resource access crucial for the Egyptian war effort.
        - UpdatedTurn: 315
        
        ### The Zulus
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Zulu war is progressing excellently (66% war score, 30% war weariness vs their 54%). Their capital Ulundi is within reach and capturing it would give us a third capital (33% domination progress). Max hostility ensures AI commits sufficient forces to finish this war quickly, freeing up 3,000+ military strength for the American front where we're under naval attack.
        - UpdatedTurn: 403
        
        ### The Huns
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: The Huns have been defeated and eliminated from the game. Resetting our maximum hostility modifiers from -100/-100 to neutral 0/0 to reflect their removal from the strategic map and reduce ongoing warmonger penalties associated with a non-existent civilization.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Byzantium has been defeated and no longer exists as a relevant player in this game. Resetting relationship modifiers from -100/-100 to neutral 0/0 to reflect their elimination and reduce unnecessary diplomatic penalties.
        - UpdatedTurn: 408
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Egypt is militarily crushed (665 strength) and offers no economic benefit. Our -100 hostility modifiers generate unnecessary warmonger penalties that hurt our World Congress standing (we lead with 16 delegates). Resetting to neutral reduces penalties while maintaining the option to capture their wonders (Louvre, Nalanda) later through India proxy wars. Egypt provides zero GPT relief, so diplomatic normalization costs us nothing while improving our global image before the India confrontation.
        - UpdatedTurn: 416
        
        ### America
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: America war is concluding with Chicago as final target. Maximum relations ensure immediate peace acceptance once captured, ending 200+ GPT war costs. With New York already secured, Chicago's fall will trigger capitulation, freeing resources for India confrontation in 8 turns. This is pure economic triage to prevent bankruptcy.
        - UpdatedTurn: 418
        
        ### India
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: India remains the existential threat to all victory paths: 62% domination vs our 37%, 4 culturally influential civs vs our 1 (and 1 turn from influencing us), Apollo Program built vs our none, and 15,340 military strength with Giant Death Robots vs our 20,047. Peace treaty expires in 2 turns - maximum hostility ensures immediate declaration and total war commitment. We cannot afford half-measures; economic crisis be damned, military victory is the only path. Their Delhi capture would instantly give us domination parity and massive gold/plunder to solve our -1222 GPT crisis.
        - UpdatedTurn: 421
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: With a catastrophic –1 021 GPT and only 379 gold in the treasury, immediate economic triage is required. Maximizing Gold (100), Happiness (100), Infrastructure (100) and Tile Improvement (100) will accelerate the construction of gold‑generating buildings, trade routes and city connections. Simultaneously boosting Diplomacy (100) and Espionage (100) helps protect our remaining city‑state allies after the recent –3 000 influence loss in Bunkeya and Zurich, and may secure extra GPT from deals. Keeping Offense high (70) and Mobilization at zero ensures we focus on war preparation without adding new unit maintenance costs. Growth (80) ensures cities work more gold‑producing tiles. This balance keeps us primed for the decisive war with India while stemming the economic bleed.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 45

        - CityDefense: 40

        - Proposals:
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)
              : Free Great Scientist.
              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: Decolonization:
            - Description: Enforce global decolonization. If successful, the Influence of all Players with all City-States is lowered to 50, unless they are permanent allies.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Egypt
          - Enact: Aztec Sphere of Influence: Lhasa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: The Aztecs
        - Contender: The Aztecs

        ### 238
        - Type: PlayerDoneTurn

        You, Montezuma (leader of The Aztecs, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 422.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      
      ## Policies
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Statecraft (New Branch):
        - Statecraft improves your ability to benefit from Trade Routes, City-States, Spies, and the World Congress.
        - Adopting Statecraft grants:
        - * +1 of every Yield in Capital for every 20 Citizens in your Empire.
        - * +1 Gold in every City.
        - Each Statecraft policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Gold in every City, and +10% Influence from Diplomatic Missions.
        - Adopting all policies in Statecraft grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Palace of Westminster.
        - * Every World Congress Session, gain 5 Culture, Science, and Gold for each Delegate you control, scaling with Era.
        - * World Wonders require 1 less unlocked Policy for every 3 City-State alliances you maintain.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Diplomats with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

This aligns our staff's tactical mindset with our goal of overrunning Shaka's weakly defended cities and exploiting the Aztec trait of gaining gold from unit kills to solve our financial crisis.

    - #1:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: -100
        - TargetID: 1
        - Rationale: Maintain maximum hostility towards India. Their peace treaty has expired, and we are launching an all-out campaign to capture Delhi, destroy their Giant Death Robots, and prevent their victory. Every Indian unit we kill will yield 150% of its strength as Gold, helping fund our empire.
        - Private: -100
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: Set maximum hostility towards the Zulus. Shaka is India's vassal and will be drawn into our war. His cities (Ulundi and Umgungundlovu) are directly adjacent to our massive troop concentrations and are extremely weakly defended. We will overrun them immediately to capture Shaka's capital and secure massive gold plunder.
        - Private: -100
        - Public: -100
        - TargetID: 5
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Refrigeration
        - Rationale: Refrigeration is selected as our next technology to extend our Trade Route range, allowing our newly created Caravans to reach deep into our vassals' (Korea and America) territories to secure vital gold income while we are under global Sanctions.
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Confirming New World Order as our next policy. It will provide a much-needed -2 Unhappiness reduction in all cities, helping us overcome the unhappiness combat penalty and ensuring our military operates at peak efficiency.
        - Policy: New World Order (Policy)

      - rationale: Pivoting our flavors to support total war. Increasing Offense (90) and Air (95) to maximize the effectiveness of our 40 Heavy Bombers and 60 Zeros in crushing Indian and Zulu forces. Maintaining Gold (100) and Happiness (100) at maximum priority to resolve our gold deficit through combat kills and city plunder, and to eliminate the happiness combat penalty. Keeping Diplomacy (90) high to protect our city-state alliances in the upcoming World Congress vote against Decolonization.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Grand Strategy**
            
            My focus is on refining our Conquest strategy in this Information Era turn. I'm reviewing current metrics and preparing for the next aggressive push, ensuring our dominance.
            
            **Analyzing Policy Options**
            
            My current thinking is centered on the imminent policy selection. I'm weighing the strategic implications of Autocracy's Level 1 options against continuing established branches, aiming to optimize our advantage given the economic and happiness constraints.
            
            **Evaluating Research Pathways**
            
            My current contemplation involves the discrepancy between the ongoing Stealth research and the listed Refrigeration option. I am analyzing the implications of prioritizing Refrigeration to unlock key advancements versus continuing with Stealth, considering the remaining turns for each.
            
            **Addressing Financial Crisis**
            
            My current focus has shifted dramatically due to a severe financial deficit. I'm scrutinizing our trade routes and recently expired peace treaty with India, trying to understand the drastic gold loss despite our military advantage.
            
            **Investigating Domination Leads**
            
            My current thinking is focused on the surprising dominance displayed by India. I'm investigating how they've managed to control five capitals, including Ulundi, and trying to decipher the relationship with the Zulus, as this could unlock a key path to victory if I can challenge them directly.
            
            **Resolving Sanctions' Impact**
            
            My immediate concern is the critical financial deficit, triggered by Aztec sanctions which have severed most trade routes. I'm analyzing our limited income from our vassals, Korea and America, to find a way to mitigate this severe gold loss and avoid unit strikes and science penalties.
            
            **Clarifying Trade Restrictions**
            
            I'm currently untangling the intricacies of the Aztec sanctions. The key issue is whether our trade routes to City-States like Kuala Lumpur and Sidon are permitted. My investigation suggests they remain active, which is a crucial detail for understanding our gold flow and potentially mitigating the deficit.
            
            **Adjusting Financial Priorities**
            
            I'm currently grappling with the steep increase in unit maintenance due to the Global Peace Accords, which has plunged me into a severe gold deficit. I'm re-evaluating our policy options, specifically Free Trade and its economic benefits, to find a viable solution to this critical financial strain.
            
            **Analyzing Trade Partners**
            
            My current focus is on understanding our GPT situation with Korea following India's departure. I've confirmed our net GPT from Korea is 93, and have analyzed potential trade with America and Egypt, noting the Zulus are at war with India and not us, which is a crucial detail.
            
            **Investigating High Maintenance**
            
            My current thinking is focused on the staggering unit maintenance costs, driven by our large, advanced military. I'm also trying to figure out how to repeal the active sanctions on the Aztecs, as the current World Congress proposals don't seem to allow for it, despite the urgent need to address our financial deficit.
            
            **Launching Aggressive War**
            
            I'm now focusing on a bold war declaration against India. Their immense wealth and our unique ability to gain substantial gold and faith from combat offer a direct solution to our current financial crisis. My analysis confirms our superior military strength makes this a viable and potentially game-winning strategy.
            
            **Leveraging Air Superiority**
            
            My current focus is on the immense power of our air fleet. With 40 Heavy Bombers and 60 Zeros/Jet Fighters, I'm assessing how to best deploy this advantage for offensive operations, potentially as a solution to our ongoing financial and strategic challenges.
            
            **Exploiting Airpower**
            
            I'm now keenly aware of our overwhelming air superiority. The strategy to declare war on India and leverage our bombers against their ground units for substantial gold generation seems incredibly promising. I'm verifying proximity to their cities to ensure this tactic is feasible.
            
            **Deploying Airpower Against India**
            
            My current focus is on leveraging our air superiority. I've pinpointed our border cities like Kabul and Texcoco as ideal staging grounds to launch heavy bombers and strike India's occupied Egyptian cities, especially Thebes, Elephantine, and Heliopolis, directly addressing our financial crisis through combat rewards.
            
            **Assessing Naval Strength**
            
            I'm reassessing our naval and air capabilities. Singapore's substantial bomber and fighter complement, combined with its naval fleet, presents a prime opportunity to strike India's eastern coast and nearby cities, potentially resolving our financial woes.
            
            **Targeting Giant Death Robot**
            
            I've identified India's Giant Death Robot in Giza. Singapore's air and naval assets are within striking distance. I'm confirming our negative diplomatic stance with India to enable maximum aggression, aiming to neutralize this threat and exploit their vulnerabilities.
            
            **Revising Diplomatic Status**
            
            My current focus is on a diplomatic oversight. I previously believed I was at war with the Zulus, but upon review, it appears we are not. I'm now clarifying our relationship status with them and the other major players like India and Korea, which has implications for my ongoing strategic assessments.
            
            **Declaring War on India**
            
            My current thinking is centered on an impending war with India, now that I understand their relationship with the Zulus. Since the Zulus voluntarily became India's vassal, their previous war with us has ended, meaning we'll be pulled into war with them automatically if we attack India. This opens up the perfect opportunity to capture Ulundi, Shaka's capital, which is conveniently located near our borders.
            
            **Assessing Zulu Threat**
            
            I'm now realizing our significant military advantage near Zulu cities Ulundi and Umgungundlovu. With our superior troop strength poised for immediate action, should war be declared on India, these Zulu capitals will be swiftly captured.
            
            **Calculating War's Profits**
            
            I'm now calculating the immense profit potential of attacking India and the Zulus. Eliminating their units, particularly the lower-tech Zulu forces and high-tech Indian armor, will flood our coffers with gold. Bombing campaigns alone promise over 1000 gold per turn.
            
            **Securing Trade Income**
            
            I'm now focusing on leveraging our vacant trade route capacity. With sanctions limiting external trade, I'm ensuring our routes to vassals like Korea and America are established to generate crucial gold income and mitigate the financial deficit.
            
            **Prioritizing Trade Routes**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing our gold generation through new caravans. I'm prioritizing trade routes to our vassals, Korea and America, as these are unaffected by sanctions and offer significant profit potential. I'm also ensuring our flavor settings correctly reflect this economic imperative.
            
            **Revising Strategic Flavors**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing our strategic flavor settings in light of the impending war. I'm adjusting Offense to 80 and Mobilization to 30, recognizing that unit supply is ample and gold generation from combat will mitigate maintenance costs, allowing us to prioritize offensive capabilities.
            
            **Optimizing Policy Choice**
            
            I'm currently reassessing our upcoming policy selection. The "New World Order" policy's significant reduction in unhappiness, especially from Distress, appears crucial for mitigating our combat penalty and boosting city happiness. I'm verifying this impact against alternative policy benefits.
            
            **Adopting "New World Order"**
            
            My current thinking has solidified on adopting the "New World Order" policy, as its happiness benefits will significantly boost unit effectiveness by mitigating combat penalties. Concurrently, I've designated Refrigeration as the next technology to research, primarily for its ability to extend trade route distances, thereby increasing our gold income from distant vassals. My diplomatic assessments also confirm the necessity of maintaining maximum hostility towards India and the Zulus.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Alignments**
            
            My current focus is on fine-tuning our diplomatic stances with key vassals like Korea and America, ensuring their continued loyalty. I'm also reassessing Egypt's status as a war-torn distraction for India, while confirming our overwhelming military superiority over the Zulus, positioning us to seize their capitals immediately upon war declaration.
            
            **Assessing Zulu Weakness**
            
            I'm now keenly aware of the Zulus' incredibly weak military presence, consisting mostly of obsolete units. Our advanced forces, including Heavy Bombers, are perfectly positioned to swiftly eliminate their army and generate substantial gold from each kill, especially from their less advanced troops.
            
            **Defending Our Ally**
            
            I'm now focusing on defending our ally, Zurich, from potential Indian aggression. India's forces are dangerously close to Zurich, but our own nearby cities of Bulawayo and Nobamba possess a substantial bomber and fighter fleet, poised to intercept any Indian advance and protect our ally.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Persona**
            
            I'm now examining our diplomatic persona settings. The goal is to increase our aggression and war focus, ensuring our staff prioritizes efficient and intelligent execution of our upcoming conflicts with India and Shaka.
            
            **Updating Diplomatic Aggression**
            
            I'm now adjusting our diplomatic persona to reflect our aggressive stance. My primary focus is increasing WarBias, Boldness, and Meanness to maximum levels, signaling our intent for total war against India and the Zulus, while reducing FriendlyBias to emphasize our self-reliance and focus on victory.
            
            **Launching War Campaign**
            
            My focus is on initiating a swift, profitable war. India's wealth and weak defenses, coupled with the Zulus' obsolescence, present a prime opportunity. My bombers and ground forces are positioned to capture their capitals and generate substantial gold, resolving our financial crisis and paving the way for victory.
            
            **Adjusting Strategic Flavors**
            
            My current focus is on optimizing our strategic flavor settings in light of the impending war. I'm adjusting Offense to 90 and Gold/Happiness to 100, recognizing our overwhelming military strength and the critical need to resolve the financial deficit. Infrastructure and Tile Improvement remain high to support our expansion.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Edge**
            
            My current focus is on leveraging our dominant position in the World Congress. With 18 delegates, we are the leading diplomatic power, and I'm prioritizing the "Aztec Sphere of Influence" proposal for Lhasa. This will solidify our alliance and prevent India from gaining influence there, further strengthening our strategic standing.
            
            **Opposing Critical Proposals**
            
            I am now focused on our diplomatic actions in the World Congress. My priority is to strongly oppose India's "International Space Station" proposal and Egypt's "Decolonization" proposal. The latter would be disastrous, wiping out our critical alliances with Lhasa, Zurich, Bunkeya, and Sogut.
            
            **Finalizing War Preparations**
            
            My current focus is on the final actions before war. This includes setting our persona to maximum aggression, formalizing our negative relationships with India and the Zulus, initiating Refrigeration research, and adopting the "New World Order" policy. My strategic flavors are now set to prioritize Offense (90), Gold/Happiness (100), and Diplomacy (90) to secure crucial city-state alliances and prepare for the upcoming World Congress.
            
            **Setting Diplomatic and Persona Levels**
            
            My focus is on finalizing diplomatic settings and persona adjustments. I am implementing negative public and private standings towards India and the Zulus, while ensuring positive relationships with our allies. Persona levels for Boldness, WarBias, and HostileBias are being maximized for aggressive conflict, with reductions in FriendlyBias and Meanness adjusted accordingly.

        - #2:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: gTq4pwORheiuupM0
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Public: -100
            - TargetID: 1
            - Rationale: Maintain maximum hostility towards India. Their peace treaty has expired, and we are launching an all-out campaign to capture Delhi, destroy their Giant Death Robots, and prevent their victory. Every Indian unit we kill will yield 150% of its strength as Gold, helping fund our empire.
            - Private: -100
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: fcY0O7gEVRUjYOFs
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Rationale: Set maximum hostility towards the Zulus. Shaka is India's vassal and will be drawn into our war. His cities (Ulundi and Umgungundlovu) are directly adjacent to our massive troop concentrations and are extremely weakly defended. We will overrun them immediately to capture Shaka's capital and secure massive gold plunder.
            - Private: -100
            - Public: -100
            - TargetID: 5
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #4:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: Bb8Mdxl4SBkYYoyD
          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Technology: Refrigeration
            - Rationale: Refrigeration is selected as our next technology to extend our Trade Route range, allowing our newly created Caravans to reach deep into our vassals' (Korea and America) territories to secure vital gold income while we are under global Sanctions.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #5:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: b7MEFDBufxfgGRz2
          - toolName: set-policy
          - input:
            - Rationale: Confirming New World Order as our next policy. It will provide a much-needed -2 Unhappiness reduction in all cities, helping us overcome the unhappiness combat penalty and ensuring our military operates at peak efficiency.
            - Policy: New World Order (Policy)
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #6:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: BLrcaXpQjuUERxGc
          - toolName: set-flavors
          - input:
            - Flavors:
              - Infrastructure: 90
              - Mobilization: 30
              - Culture: 70
              - Happiness: 100
              - CityDefense: 40
              - Mobile: 60
              - Ranged: 80
              - Offense: 90
              - Wonder: 20
              - UseNuke: 50
              - Air: 95
              - Diplomacy: 90
              - Defense: 40
              - TileImprovement: 90
              - Espionage: 100
              - Science: 80
              - GreatPeople: 30
              - Gold: 100
              - Growth: 70
              - Nuke: 95
            - GrandStrategy: Conquest
            - Rationale: Pivoting our flavors to support total war. Increasing Offense (90) and Air (95) to maximize the effectiveness of our 40 Heavy Bombers and 60 Zeros in crushing Indian and Zulu forces. Maintaining Gold (100) and Happiness (100) at maximum priority to resolve our gold deficit through combat kills and city plunder, and to eliminate the happiness combat penalty. Keeping Diplomacy (90) high to protect our city-state alliances in the upcoming World Congress vote against Decolonization.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
